ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law contains amendments to the Health Information System Act concerning, in particular, provisions on medical registers. The aim of these changes is to reflect the fact that in practice medical registers are created mainly at the request of the entities to which they are subsequently entrusted. Medical records are used, among others. for the purpose of carrying out scientific and research and development activities. Consequently, it is proposed to place the controller at the above level. entities. At the same time, registers were obliged to transmit data from medical records free of charge at the request of the Minister responsible for Health and the supervisory and control powers of that Minister were maintained. The catalogue of entities entrusted with the operation of medical records has been extended to include the National Health Fund. The draft law shall not be required to present to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union, including the European Central Bank, for the purpose of obtaining an opinion, notification, consultation or agreement. The bill will not affect the activities of micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises. The proposed law does not contain technical provisions within the meaning of the provisions of the Council of Ministers Regulation of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts and is therefore not subject to notification. There are no alternative measures to the adoption of the proposed law to achieve the intended objective. The bill submitted is not contrary to European Union law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u31	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please, Mrs Józef Hrynkiewicz, the Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u32	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Health Information System Act and some other laws, print No 1687. The aim of the contested law is to amend and clarify the Law of 28 April 2011. [[Game in the O.R.]] Marshal, may I have your peace of mind? The aim of the contested law is to amend and clarify the Health Information System Act of 28 April 2011, which includes provisions on medical records created by the Minister of Health. The need for amendments to the Law cited in 2011 is due to the health registers created in the health registers under the authority of the Minister of Health. The creation of new medical registers and their changes in scope and form result from the development of research, information needs in the health management process and the dissemination of new information and communication techniques. In view of the above considerations, the Constitutional Tribunal has approved the establishment of medical registers by means of a regulation, allowing the Health Minister to respond flexibly to emerging new needs related to the creation of medical registers. In its judgment of 18 December 2014, the Constitutional Tribunal also approved the application of the Regulation to entities determining the keeping of medical records. The proposed law also clarifies the rules on financing rules and the financing of medical registers. The Act also establishes a catalogue of entities to which the Minister of Health may entrust the keeping of the register. Currently Minister of Health under Article. 20 laws have created six medical records. These are the National Cancer Register kept by the oncology centre, the register of non-malicious cancers kept by the clinical hospital in Poznań, the register of acute coronary syndromes kept by the Silesian Heart Disease Centre in Zabrze, the National Register of Cardiosurgical Operations which runs the Children's Health Centre in Warsaw, the Medical Register of Supported Procreation and the register of congenital malformations maintained by the Medical University in Poznań. This project introduces the obligation for providers, public registers and medical registers to transfer data free of charge to medical records. The Minister shall, by means of a regulation, identify the above mentioned entities. The draft law clarifies the definition of electronic medical records. The draft amends a number of laws, the scope of which concerns the scope of which is governed by the law of scope. The Civil Status Act 2014 extends the transitional period for extending until 1 January 2023 to statistical services by the heads of civil status offices the data contained in birth cards, dead birth cards and death cards. This solution, in my opinion, requires the Minister to explain to the High House why this term is so much longer. This extension, once again, requires a clear explanation, as the lack of this data significantly affects the quality of demographic statistics published by the CSO. The explanation in the justification is, in my opinion, insufficient. The draft law was subject to a wide public consultation, which resulted in changes in the draft law being taken into account. I would also like to stress that this change, which is being introduced, will allow more registers to be made so that there is more information that will significantly facilitate health management and which can significantly and significantly affect the rational spending of resources, the running of health programmes. This is an extremely necessary law, which is why the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of this law without any amendments. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u62	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! I would like to present the following information in the discussion. First, I will refer to what you said at the Modern Club. Namely, this project creates legal instruments to ensure that registers are financed by grants. The provision that you have invoked explains how the register can be financed under health policy programmes, and such registers are already funded at the moment. Professor Zembala mentioned it. There is also a very important register, the National Register of Cardiosurgical Operations, which is also funded under health policy programmes. Here, on the other hand, this law creates a legal provision for the Minister of Health to also finance these registers by way of grants. And that's the important thing for the budget registers to be financed. This is one important thing. The second important point that you have pointed out is that the register or registers can be kept - formally the register creates a health minister through a regulation - by the NFZ, because the National Health Fund has been included in the list of entities that can keep registers. And you have already noticed that this is a very important change in this bill. This will allow the reporting data collected by the National Health Fund to be combined with analytical data related to the conduct of medicinal activities, with details. The Act creates mechanisms for the National Health Fund to be able to receive data from the registers that are currently being kept. Moreover, we have a situation at the moment that many registers actually work on a voluntary basis, i.e. the part of the entities provides the data and the part does not. It doesn't create a full picture. The Act creates a mechanism for certain entities indicated in the Regulation to transfer data to registers for payment. Thanks to this, we are creating a comprehensive system of full imaging of certain areas of information, and also using such a mechanism that it is the National Health Fund that has the opportunity to receive these data indicated by itself in a specific procedure, we are creating a system that allows us to capture... here one of your messengers spoke about this, perhaps Mrs Zwierzan, that there is no system that sees the whole. It is through this mechanism outlined in this bill that we create such a system, because the National Health Fund is the largest source of data that we have right now. If we correct the analytical data from the medical records, we should get the right result. Another point that has been highlighted in the discussion is the issue of time shifting. Ladies and gentlemen, the implementation of the P1 project so far has failed. It ended in failure because the project unfortunately had bad assumptions, it was simply too big. Too much was wanted at the same time, at the same time such a method that certain stages, and not even stages, only certain products within this project, were implemented by various companies. This involved the misconception that in order to carry out this project, public procurement needs to be shredded in some way so that the public procurement can be carried out. That was years ago. And indeed these public procurements were successful because large IT companies took part in this project, only that it could not be tightened, it was not possible to short-circuit this project. And that's why this project fell over. At the moment, the method is to do it in stages, i.e. strictly products. And that's why we're talking about a schedule: only if we do one thing, we'll do another. And from here, for example. extending the deadline for the implementation of electronic documents that operate under the Civil Status Act, because first we do systemic things, i.e. prescription, referral, electronic medical documentation, i.e. what affects the system's life cycle, and only when we do so, we will do the next things. Doing everything at once led to a fall. This method had to be changed, and at the moment this project is being introduced by a method called agile or agile method, it is that CSiOZ buys de facto works and organises the works itself. At the moment, the project is carried out on the basis of an agreement with the Ministry of Digitalization, and thanks to the introduction of such milestones and the distribution of the entire huge task into smaller stages, this project should be carried out at the time it was presented here. As far as safety is concerned, the definition of safety and security are set out in the files that directly regulate it. There is a direct appeal in this law and a name for what level of security should be maintained. I hope that my answers will be sufficient for you. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Our country has extremely poor water resources, and the efficiency of its use is at a low level, even lower than e.g. in sunny Spain. Water scarcity is a problem for many sectors of the economy, but it is particularly dangerous for agriculture, as water is a decisive factor in the size and quality of the crops. A small retention is the solution to the problem of field stepping. Unfortunately, the importance of a small retention for agriculture was not appreciated, which resulted in wet areas, ponds or old-fashioned land that occurred among the fields, being largely drained, converted into arable land or green pastures. I hope that the new Water Law will allow more protection of available water resources. The amendments tabled by the Senate would like to make a positive assessment. The amendment aimed at reducing the level of the upper rates for the collection of water for agricultural purposes, for the supply of water to people and economic animals in areas which are not normal use of water, and for the purpose of carrying out the municipalities' own tasks in the field of collective supply of water for human consumption, deserves particular recognition. This is an amendment I asked for twice or even three times. I'm glad she was accepted by the Senate. Agricultural activity in Poland is currently at the verge of cost-effectiveness, so we cannot allow it to be further increased as it threatens to lose competitiveness in foreign agricultural markets. This situation applies not only to agriculture but also to the agri-food industries for which water is needed. To conclude, I would like to express my hope that the new Water Law, through rational management of water resources, will lead to an increase in water resources. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u224	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for giving me the floor. I stand before you today to once again express my concern about the draft law on the Supreme Court. The Supreme Court Act, as well as the other two laws: on the National Council of Courts and the Law on the System of General Courts, will have one effect - they will deprive citizens of the right to an independent court, one of the most important civil rights, a right without which other rights and freedoms cannot be defended, without which authorities cannot be controlled without which power cannot be restricted. The essence of the right to court is the independence of the judiciary, independence from politicians, powerful businessmen, large companies of the Treasury, local barons and political agreements. The judge is to rule on the basis of constitution and laws. It is the duty of the State to create for him such service conditions that he will never be in a situation of subjection. He must have a free mind so that he can always serve the citizens and their affairs in a dedicated way. Many organizations and institutions protest against the Supreme Court Act. Among them are professional self-governments, judicial associations, Polish NGOs, trade unions. The President of the European Parliament, the Secretary-General of the Council of Europe, wrote to the Marshal of the Sejm, and he would also like to present his opinion to the Organisation for Security and Cooperation in Europe in due time. These are not just voices of opposition, they are voices of concern for our country, about what our future will look like, whether we will be the same partner for cooperation as we have been for many years - the predictable, stable, reform-oriented state, but the dedicated idea that the law cannot be broken, that the process of changing the law is subject to the same standards as the doctor's work, the construction work, that those who are the law, like doctors whether builders do not make decisions too rashly, without checking, hearing others, diagnosing, detailed research, because only in this way can faults be eliminated, and finally faults affect the weakest. It is also a concern whether, as a Member State of the European Union, we will be able to be treated as a party that can be trusted. All judicial cooperation in criminal and civil and family matters is based on mutual trust. It is about criminal matters when Poland asks for the release of a criminal who has fled the country somewhere on the sunny beaches of Spain, but it is also about the custody of a child when one of the parents lives in Poland and the other in Ireland. If there is no principle of mutual trust between justice systems, unfortunately, this cooperation will not go well. In my opinion, the amendments proposed by your President and the amendments which have been the subject of the committee's work have unfortunately not changed much. Moreover, they are deepening the state of legal chaos, which became the subject of my opinion, which was presented today to the Marshal of the Sejm. And since this is an issue that will have a significant impact on how the National Judicial Council and the Supreme Court will be set up in the future, let me present some of this opinion. The participation of the Sejm in the creation of the personal composition of the National Judicial Council is defined in Article. 187 constitutions. As we know, it is limited to the choice of four members of the National Court of Justice from among the Members. On the other hand, in the light of the article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 point 2 of the Constitution are the judges themselves who should elect 15 members of the National Courts from among the judges of the Supreme Court, common courts, etc. In this way, the principle of separation and independence of the judicial authority is implemented. Deficiencies of the amendment, which consists in the addition of an article. 9a to the Act on the National Judicial Council, is particularly bright in the light of the transitional provisions of the proposed law on the Supreme Court. According to Art. 92. 3 of this project The National Judicial Board shall examine and evaluate the candidates for judges of the Supreme Court and shall submit proposals to the President for the appointment of candidates for judges of the Supreme Court within 14 days of the date of submission of the nomination. In the light of the amendment proposing a new wording for the article. 92. 4 of the project, in the event of an unsuccessful expiry of the deadline to submit a request for the appointment of a candidate for the position of a Supreme Court judge to the President of the Republic of Poland, it is sufficient to adopt a resolution of one of the Assembly of the National Judicial Council on the issuing of a positive assessment of the candidate. What does that mean in practice? That means in the light of art. 21b added by the Act of 12 July amending the Act on the National Court Register to present candidates for the positions of Supreme Court judges to the President of the Republic of Poland, it is enough to pass a resolution of the first meeting of the Council consisting of the Minister of Justice and the first President of the Supreme Court, the President of the Supreme Administrative Court, the President of the President and four members elected by the Sejm and two members elected by the Senate from among the senators, i.e. the so-called. a political chamber or a political assembly of the National Judicial Council. In fact, the amendment aims to ensure that candidates for Supreme Court judges are actually represented by a dominating assembly of the National Courts, as evidenced by its personal composition, by a political factor. In the light of these amendments, politicians will decide on the staffing of the Supreme Court. The amendment to add an article should be taken into account. 9a to the Act on the National Judicial Council. It requires the selection of members of the National Courts by a majority of 3/5 votes in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of Members in combination with those provided for in Article. 92 § 3 of the draft short, because the 14-day deadline for the new National Council of the Court of Justice to assess candidates for the positions of Supreme Court judges will very likely lead to the failure of the National Court of Justice to carry out such an assessment as an integral body within that period. This is because, as a result of the lack of political agreement, the majority of 3/5 votes required by the proposed amendment to the selection of the judiciary of the KRS will not be reached. The proposed amendments may therefore lead to a meeting of the National Courts of Justice not being able to participate in the appointment of new judges to the Supreme Court, as it will not be elected at all by then. In fact, the Supreme Court will therefore be a court chosen by a body dominated by politicians, which is only a part of the National Judicial Council. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u358	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendment we are looking for, among other things, partial exclusion of the application of the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure from the process of awarding the scientific category to scientific bodies. Similarly, with regard to the principles of the allocation of funds by the National Centre for Research and Development and the National Centre for Science. The amendment is intended to improve the process of awarding the scientific category and the funding for projects financed by the NCBR and the NCN. It is also intended to eliminate doubts as to the legal nature of the proceedings carried out in the context of the award of the scientific category by the Minister and in both centres. Certain provisions of the c.p.a. will not apply to the procedures for the award of the scientific category to the scientific unit. Those provisions assume that public administrations must ensure that parties participate actively in any stage of the procedure and must allow the parties to comment on the evidence and material collected prior to the decision. The PSL Club believes that this is a good direction, so we will support the proposed changes.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u372	Thank you very much, Senator. Senator! High Izbo! I have the honour to introduce...
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u373	Excuse me, Professor, but we're already in the Seym, so...
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u374	I'm sorry. Excuse me. Dear Members, I'm sorry, but I was in the Senate, and that's why it's a slip. I have the honour to present you with a report on the activities of the Supreme Court on behalf of this court. I would like to say that it is up to the Supreme Court to recognize, in addition to the cassations and supervision of Judaism, also electoral protests, the validity of elections to the Sejm and Senate, to the European Parliament, and the election of the President of the Republic of Poland. At the moment, the Supreme Court also rules on the validity of the national referendum. In addition, it is currently giving its opinion on the draft laws and other normative acts on the basis of which the courts rule and function, as well as on the drafts of other laws in so far as it considers it appropriate. In the law which was passed by the Sejm, the Supreme Court was deprived of this function, although it was highly praised by both Chambers for their opinions - insightful and very substantive. Each of these areas of activity of the Supreme Court is a separate perspective of its assessment. The primary task of the Supreme Court is, of course, the execution of the judicial function. In this context, cases were less affected than in the previous year by 100. A total of 11 102 cases were brought in. Cassation complaints and cassations prevaild - a total of 7886. The largest number of cassation complaints, as many as 3492, were brought to the Civil Chamber, while to the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs - 2567, to the Criminal Chamber - 1821. 1146 complaints were also received and 164 legal issues were presented. In 2015, there were 173 issues. The other cases include appeals, actions for the determination of the legality of final decisions, competition and consumer cases, complaints for the length of proceedings, actions for reopening, requests for referrals to another court, cases for pardons and case-laws, as well as disciplinary matters. In the latter group of cases - she is certainly interested in the High Chamber - the Supreme Court has ruled 62 times on disciplinary liability of judges, and in the case of other groups, legal advisers, lawyers, notaries, prosecutors, doctors, pharmacists, 90 cases have been dealt with. Compared to the previous year, the difference between the cases brought and those identified is more favourable. While 712 cases were considered less than received in 2015, more than 325 cases were identified in 2016. The waiting time for a substantive examination of the case - that is also in the interest of the High House - is on average 7 months. In 2016 there was a lower impact of cases to the Military Chamber of the Supreme Court. This trend may be reduced in such a way that steps are taken to restore the jurisdiction of military courts regulated for a transitional period in Article. 12 Laws introducing the Code of Criminal Procedure, since the structure of the criminal justice and prosecution authorities in military matters has been preserved and continues to function, only for a limited number of cases to be considered. What is the organisation of work in court? The number of posts is set at 93. As of 31 December 2016, on average, 83 judges were adjudicated. The Supreme Court has periodically benefited from the assistance of four judges appointed by the Minister of Justice at the request of the first President. One of the most important aspects of the organizational activity was the dissemination of information on the case law of the Supreme Court. The actions taken were reflected in all publications that are published and which are also, colloquially speaking, suspended on the website. Each year there were trips, scientific conferences. The Supreme Court also held a scientific exchange with the Supreme Courts. In 2016, it was primarily the Slovak Republic. As in previous years, via the Press Team, the Supreme Court received requests for access to case files, audio and video recordings in media matters, information on timing, trial proceedings, etc. All of this has been greatly improved - this whole information sphere in the Supreme Court - and it already works without criticism of the media, which we note with great pleasure. The case-law of each Chamber concerns a very diverse group of issues. The main issues are the complexity of the legal problems examined and the repeated recourse in case law to international law, European law, the case law of the European Court of Human Rights and the Court of Justice. This creates some difficulties and prolongs the examination of cases, as it requires analysis not only of national law but also of European standards. The most important were judgments of this kind, of such an important nature, in the Civil Chamber. This chamber very often refers to international standards, because this is its object, civil law most often comes into contact with international law. For example, two resolutions can be indicated. One concerned the insolvency of the travel agency and the other concerned the re-registration of the bankruptcy. The Supreme Court has dealt with these matters in the context of European directives. Here we can say, by the way, that the quality of the implementation of the directives should be slightly improved in the legal order, because then there would be no need to rule also in the context of the directives, and it would be possible to focus on Polish law. A major problem was last year with regard to the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal and the application of non-promulgated judgments, which were however announced. In this context, the Supreme Court has indicated that these judgments have jurisdictional effect. The legislative threads also appeared in other judgments than the V CZ 86/16, which I mentioned. It was considered e.g. in the V CSK 371/15, the exclusive jurisdiction of the Constitutional Tribunal as regards the finding of non-compliance with the Constitution, but at the same time it was assumed that a pro-constitutional interpretation could be made if the legal solution is similar to that already derogated by the Constitutional Tribunal. Those decisions that concern citizens' rights are important, and here the Supreme Court often referred to constitutional standards, e.g. has stated that the provisions of the Basic Law require the Court of Justice to provide information on the determination of injury and the related modification of evidence. This was the case in Case V CSK 294/15. The importance of constitutional standards has been the subject of many judgments, including the V CSK 455/15, which points out that the Directive in dubio pro libertate applies to the legal text relating to individual freedoms and rights. In accordance with this principle, none of the individual cases of citizens can be resolved in a way that violates the obligation to respect human dignity, i.e. the Judaism concerning the protection of citizens' rights. Many judgments signalled noticeable legislative shortcomings, but this is what is mentioned in the information about perceived imperfections, which is why I will not speak more closely in this part of my paper. In the activities of the Civil Chamber, the issue of liability for damages and insurance contracts is very important. She appears every year in our information about Supreme Court rulings. This year, a conference was held on the determination of the damage and liability of the insurer. The Supreme Court, on the other hand, held in 2016 that the insurers' benefits under the compulsory insurance contract for motor vehicle owners also cover the costs of treatment and rehabilitation not financed by public funds. It was also a judgment which met with great approval of the insured. Of course, the greatest difficulties are related to the civil procedure. A very interesting and important solution was the protocol. It was assumed that the transcription of the protocol was not an official document and that if the content of the activities could not be determined on the basis of audio-video recording, these activities should be repeated to the appropriate extent. From the point of view of the citizens, the issue of the enforcement of judicial costs, as well as resolutions on bailiffs and the possibility of charging these services with VAT, are invaluable. This issue has been resolved by the composition of the three judges, but it will still be the subject of the question, the composition of the seven judges, and it must also be the subject of the Supreme Administrative Court, because it concerns VAT. For the time being, the Supreme Court considered that the amount of the enforcement fee could increase the amount of the enforcement fee of the service, i.e. the question was whether it could increase the bailiff's value for money, and it was found that it could not, but this is the seventh resolution. We are often faced with a pro-Union interpretation also in these judgments. As regards the ruling of the Criminal Chamber, this ruling was of great interest to Members, which concerned the interpretation of the concept of "next person" - a resolution of the composition of seven judges I KZP 20/15. I would like to point out that this resolution is only procedural, not material. The question was whether a person living together was also a person of the same sex. The Supreme Court found that gender variation is not a condition for recognition as persons living together. This involved refusing to answer, testifying. In the view of the Supreme Court, the expenses incurred by the accused, if he has been partially released from the charge, allow the Court of Justice to claim part of the reimbursement of those charges from the State Treasury - and KZP/16 speaks of it - in the part in which he was acquitted. As regards the caselaw of the Chamber of Labour, I would like to say that most of the problems arise with social security. This House is ruling to the greatest extent, precisely in the area of social security, because these rules are very often changing and the courts are not always able to deal with inter-temporal rules, and they are also quite casuistic and this also raises an interpretation problem. Interestingly, the Supreme Court in this Chamber has given effect to the legal principle of two resolutions of this court, which it does very rarely and rather in cases of trial. First, the claim of the creator of the invention for remuneration. The admissibility of the cassation action is determined by the value of the subject matter of appeal provided for in labour law cases, i.e. it is wider. In its resolution III UZP 2/16, however, it was considered that the minimum rates depend on the value of the subject matter of the dispute as regards the existence or non-existence of the social security relationship or the scope of the social security coverage. There were some judgments concerning the Post-Most Pension Fund and there was such a sensitive question in 2016, described in the press, whether a contribution should be paid for part-time employees for this Post-Most Pension Fund. The Supreme Court has decided that this is an employer's obligation. But of course we also have labour law issues. In Resolution III of the PZP 2/16, the Supreme Court answered in a affirmative manner whether a public school teacher who takes over this school as a natural person could declare his refusal to move to that school and obtain the right to check-in. Here, after long legal diwags, the Supreme Court decided that he could get this briefing. The Military Chamber is very few judgments, but there is still a little left, because this scope of cognition of the Military Chamber is shrinking, which does not mean that this Chamber and the judges of this Chamber are, colloquially speaking, unemployed because they are ruling in the Criminal Chamber. Those are the prisons. In short, by presenting this report, I would like to say that the statistical increase in legal issues considered by the Supreme Court and the gravity of the interpretation problems raised entitles me to conclude that its legislative activity is essential in the activities of the Supreme Court. There has been a slight increase in the number of provisions to refuse to adopt a resolution. The reasons for this are different - either in the court's opinion, it has been resolved or not, but it is not so important that the Supreme Court decides on this issue. These are the answers to the court's questions. The practice of broad justification in these resolutions was continued in order to present the problem holistically. This finds an applause of science and practice, because the whole particular issue is discussed in this judgment. The shortcomings of legal regulations were also signalled by the de lege ferenda. This is also an important issue, these demands are in this separate study. I would like to say that the activity of the Supreme Court is not characterised by any laxity of the proceedings, no one has ever made such a conclusion about the Supreme Court. The average case review in the Supreme Court is, like I said, seven months. This is a very good average against the background of European countries. The Military Chamber improves, sir, a little bit, but it doesn't do everything, and in the Criminal Chamber it is four months. In any case, there is absolutely no chronicity of proceedings and therefore the result of the activity of all employees of the Supreme Court, judges above all, but also assistants, is very good, substantive and about the speed of proceedings, and organizationally. I would like to say that, in total, the report presented by me in front of the High House today shows that there are no real accusations concerning the inability of the Supreme Court, the chronicity and improper professional preparation of the Supreme Court judges. As for these imperfections, I'm sending you back... I can, of course, refer to it. It's about the findings of irregularities and gaps in the law. They are based not only on the observations of the Supreme Court judges, the Office of the Court of First Instance, but also on district and appeal judges, to whom we ask each year for such information, which they believe needs to be improved. Thank you very much. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u390	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Madam President! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the activities of the Supreme Court in 2016. Today, however, is a special day - a black day for the independence of the Polish judiciary. A few hours ago, the Sejm, or more specifically, the majority of the Sejm in connection with the ruling party, subjected the independent justice system of the governing authority, breaking the constitution repeatedly. And ironically, today we are considering information about the activities of the Supreme Court in 2016. The power to consider this information is influenced by everything that has happened today. Turning to the substantive part, I would like to say that the Supreme Court, according to the Constitution, is now the highest judicial authority in Poland, a power separate and independent from other authorities. The Supreme Court, as a judicial authority, is called upon to exercise justice by ensuring the legality and uniformity of the case law of the general courts and of the military. This supervision shall be carried out through the recognition of cassation and other remedies and the adoption of resolutions determining legal issues. A very important function of the Supreme Court is the recognition of electoral protests, the validity of elections to the Sejm and Senate, the European Parliament, and the election of the President of the Republic of Poland. The Supreme Court also states that a national referendum and a constitutional referendum are valid. Speaking broadly about the powers of the Supreme Court, I would like to draw the attention of the High House to the considerable reduction of those powers which occurred in this House today when the new law on the Supreme Court was passed. I will refer to these changes later on. In the past year, as in previous years, the Supreme Court has carried out its tasks very well. In the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, during the examination of the information submitted by the first President of the Supreme Court, Professor Małgorzata Gersdorf, none of the members of the committee submitted critical comments. There were questions that were answered. I stress this mainly because several days after the meeting of the committee, a draft law on the Supreme Court was incorporated into the Parliament, whose justification was based largely on the criticism of the body's activities, its slowness, the poor organisation of work. This report does not support this criticism. These opinions are not in line with the material contained in the report. As to the information, I would like to stress that a total of 11 100 cases were brought to the Supreme Court in the reporting year, including a majority of which were cassation and cassation complaints, 1146 complaints, and 164 legal issues were presented to the Supreme Court. Other cases include appeals against military decisions of district courts, actions for finding that a final decision is unlawful, competition and consumer cases, actions for chronicity, actions for referring the case to another court and other incidental applications. It should be stressed that cassation complaints, which represented more than 70% the affected cases will not be dealt with by the Supreme Court. Today, it is not entirely clear who will be handling these matters. And yet the citizens are waiting, there are time limits for trial for the coming months. Isn't that important? How can we ignore Poles and their affairs, which are often life's affairs for them? Disciplinary matters constituted a small activity of the Supreme Court. In 2016, 61 cases concerning disciplinary offences of general court judges were brought before the Supreme Court, i.e. at a level close to the previous years. These figures contradict exaggerated and disseminated information about the wide phenomenon of the bad reputation of judges, which is not only false but very harmful, and even threatening to build citizens' confidence in the courts and established law. In the new law, the Disciplinary Chamber will be the most important. How will the number of disciplinary matters suddenly increase? Who's gonna be punished? It's a kind of pressure on the judge's environment and a kind of intimidation. How else do you understand that? This is no way to build trust in courts and judges. It's already been done, and no one responsible reaches out to such methods. In 2016, the Supreme Court dealt with 11 427 cases, 1151 complaints and 165 legal issues. Compared to 2015, more cases were identified than affected, which is a positive phenomenon. The reasons for the amendments to the Law on the Supreme Court were very often referred to the length of proceedings. What do the numbers look like? This is the waiting time for a substantive examination of the case in the Supreme Court was on average 7 months, including: in the Civil Chamber - 9 months, in the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs - 12 months, in the Criminal Chamber - 6 months and in the Military Chamber - from 1 to 2 months. These are very good results, fully in line with the standards set out in the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms and compared to those in the European Union. It's wrong, but I think it's intentional... This is another misguided, but deliberate argument that gave rise to a revolution in the Supreme Court. This information, very well dedicating the activities of the Supreme Court, is evidence of professionalism, a high level of performance of tasks and the undisputed authority of the Supreme Court in Poland. Today we should say and do this on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, which is now under the Supreme Court: Thank you for your activities in 2016. We thank the first President of the Supreme Court, Professor Margaret Gersdorf, and we thank the Supreme Court judges, as well as all the employees of this authority. It is my speech today, on the day of the vote of an unconstitutional project destroying the independence of the Supreme Court, that has a particular pronunciation. It is a kind of funeral speech over an independent judicial authority, which was and for a short time will be the Supreme Court in Poland. It is regrettable that an unprecedented procedure should be adopted to process a project of a systemic nature, a lack of consultation, including in relation to the environments of justice. It is a great loss that the government and PiS Members, the authors of the Supreme Court project, did not listen to the highest authorities in the field of law, outstanding experts in this matter, of which we should not only be proud, but draw on their knowledge and scientific achievements. Among them are Prof. Adam Strzembosz, the great authority and creator of the justice system since the time of Solidarity, the first President of the Supreme Court in free Poland. The adoption of a new law on the Supreme Court means irreparable farewell to the article. The 10th Constitution, which states: The Government of the Republic of Poland is based on the division and balance of executive authority, legislative authority and judicial authority. The legislative authority is exercised by the Sejm and the Senate, by the President of the Republic of Poland and by the Council of Ministers, and by courts and tribunals. So goodbye to the three divisions of power, the essential attribute of a democratic rule of law. To conclude my speech, once again I thank the first President of the Supreme Court, all the Supreme Court judges for their activities in 2016, but also for the activities they carry out in the Supreme Court, for their high commitment to the independence of the justice system in Poland. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

The chronicle of the Szumański family, covering the years 1720 and 1946, interested the director of the Nysa museum as the only known comprehensive family chronicle of one of many families settled in Nysa after World War II. Mr Jacek Szumański in his chronicle gathered the fate of all known family members, among whom were also participants of important historical events. The Bar Confederation, the Krakow Uprising in 1846, the January Uprising, the fight for independence, including participation in the Versailles Conference - are just some of the events of the former history in which members of the Shuman family participated. In turn, the story of the newer one, which touched this family, were the troops on Sybir, the army of General Władysław Anders, the fight in the AK and the WiN Association, and Katyń, where Stanisław Szumański, the uncle of the author of the book was murdered. One can say that the exhibition and the book reflect the complicated and tragic fates of many Polish families. Care for your family is not just about securing your current needs. It is also a concern to preserve the identity of each of these families, i.e. their individual history. For some time there has been considerable activity in Poland of people looking for their roots. They often focus on genealogical societies. Members of these associations are not only engaged in searching for documents concerning them. Very often they support state archives in document digitisation processes, making socially indexing and scanning documents. It is worth supporting this quite large social movement. Let the example of Mr.Jack Szumański encourage others to discover and describe family fates, because these individual stories make up the history of our entire nation. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u410	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Today I would like to present to you the problems faced by many Polish families - families of children with type 1 diabetes. This incurable disease has affected children since their youngest years of life, with its presence it often breaks families and causes them to poverty. Type 1 diabetes in children is a disease of the whole family. Parents, mostly mothers, have responsibility for the health and life of their children. The lack of statistics does not allow this community to be accurately represented in numbers. We probably have about 30,000 in Poland. Small and young - up to the age of 18 - diabetics, and this trend is growing. Diabetes is a non-infectious epidemic of the 21st century, a social disease, and type 1 of this disease is not caused by a bad diet or lack of movement. The family community of type 1 diabetic children constantly struggles for a complete refund of elementary needs, such as. insulin, disposable pen needles and puncturers, insulin pump containers and continuous monitoring of glycaemic control systems that help to correct subsequent complications. Continuous monitoring of glycaemic control should be performed by all patients up to at least 18 years of age and not - as today - only those selected. Diabetes is considered to be a disability, although the law used by disability boards is far from reality. Mothers who resign from work for 24 hours of care for a terminally ill child often remain without nursing and caring benefits because the committees do not notice the problems affecting them. The fight in court on the basis of care and maintenance creates unnecessary stress, generates costs for the state budget and does not promote diabetic therapy. A child with type 1 diabetes seemingly healthy is, however, at every moment of his life, day and night, at risk of loss of consciousness due to hypoglycaemia, which may end tragically. You can't burden a little man with a burden of disease by stealing from his childhood. It is the parent who should decide his maturity, not the juror who issues the ruling on the basis of the file and a few minutes' inspection. Another problem is the education of school and preschool teachers. Training education does not, unfortunately, produce the expected results. Bagatizing the subject's disease forces parents to squawk on school and preschool corridors. All this in the care of the normal childhood of their children, as far as possible, as well as their colleagues. School nurses who should take over this care during their stay in educational establishments have limited working time or are not available because the school cannot afford to hire them. The law governing these issues should be revised and adapted to today's needs and current realities. The problem can be quickly controlled by the inclusion of type 1 diabetes in a group of specially taught diseases, with emphasis being placed on specific assistance in the form of observation of the student and appropriate response in the event of unexpected decreases or increases in glycaemic levels rather than on the form of education. Every child feels pain, every parent suffers by deliberately hurting his child in order to help him. Notice in our actions also those physically and mentally exhausted parents - they are unconditionally fighting for the health and life of their children. They don't need monuments and glory, they just need a simple human instinct to understand and help from above. Let's start thinking about these families as a priority in the departmental work. These type 1 diabetes kids are tomorrow's pillar of our nation. Let us show that we can do better tomorrow for those who have not chosen this disease, they have not acquired it through their own negligence, and they bravely endure its hardships and inconveniences. Ladies and gentlemen! I am the vice-president of the Parliamentary Group for: diabetes and I promise that I will monitor what is happening in the Polish government, and I will try my best to take care of these small and later adult diabetic patients. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u411	I hope you will succeed, and today I thank you for your statement. And the last statement will be made by Lidia Gądek, the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u412	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am sorry and thank you very much for adding me to the list of speakers. I was going to, but unfortunately, I had to run a little bit back from the Presidential Palace. I support the Member, the vice-president of the team, whom I have the honour to manage thanks to the joint election, and I want us to take care of Poles together, about the health of Poles. On the other hand, I would like to make my statement on another occasion. Ladies and gentlemen! Today is a very sad day. And because Marshal Brudziński wouldn't allow it. I would like to say this now in a statement. Ladies and gentlemen of all benches, your place is not here right now. There's over 100,000 in there. The Poles are waiting for you. [[Member, we are working.]] They're waiting for you, they wanted to talk to you, they wanted to talk to us. I know it's not the state, it's the Kukiz club's representative who blocked the break for a meeting with citizens, a man who belongs to a club known as "Citizen's Movement." You were afraid to go to the citizens to tell you that today you broke all the principles of democracy and free Poland, all of them possible. Now go out to the Sejm and listen. Listen to what the Poles have to say and they have a lot to say. As I say, I'm going back from the Presidential Palace to say this and to sound like a parliamentary speaker. Poles have a lot to tell you. And not - as some say - old and barkers. Today there are hundreds of thousands of young Poles in Krakowskie Przedmieście and at the Sejm, with small children. They're 18, teenage, and they're also seniors. All of Poland, the representation of the whole nation came to tell you: stop, stop dictatorship. They don't agree to that. It's today and sad and big day. Sad, because today you have broken all the legal rules that apply in a democratic state, but great because Poles have woken up. Come out for a moment, stand in silence, if you do not want to appear, and listen to what the Poles have to say to you. And it's worth hearing. If you said you were going to listen to the Poles, why didn't you let us go to the Krakow Suburbs today or go out on your own? Shame, ladies and gentlemen. I know many of you, I respect you, but you promised Poles that you would listen to them. Today you broke the constitution that was passed by the majority of the nation. Today, you cut a million signatures on the educational referendum. Today you have practically destroyed a quarter of a million citizens' signatures in terms of health pay. Come to your senses. Come to your senses. Today it must have sounded out of that podium. Thank you very much, Marshal, again. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-20-sejm-46-3.u413	You're welcome. That concludes our parliamentary statements. Information about the impact of the interpellation, queries and answers to them is available in the Sejm Information System. This concludes the 46th session of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. The minutes of the sitting will be submitted to the Secretariat of the Sejm. The agenda for the 47th session of the Sejm, appointed on 12, 13, 14 and 15 September 2017, will be served to the ladies and gentlemen. We're adjourned.
